<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-right" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Quibcardinaux2.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/5d/Quibcardinaux2.jpg/220px-Quibcardinaux2.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="136" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/5d/Quibcardinaux2.jpg/330px-Quibcardinaux2.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/5d/Quibcardinaux2.jpg/440px-Quibcardinaux2.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1280" data-file-height="791" /></a><figcaption>1759 became known as the "<a href="/wiki/Annus_Mirablis" class="mw-redirect" title="Annus Mirablis">Annus mirabilis</a>" after Britain enjoyed victories on several continents as well as at sea. In November a French fleet <a href="/wiki/Planned_French_Invasion_of_Britain_(1759)" class="mw-redirect" title="Planned French Invasion of Britain (1759)">planning to invade England</a> was defeated at <a href="/wiki/Battle_of_Quiberon_Bay" title="Battle of Quiberon Bay">Quiberon</a>. Much of the credit went to Pitt, rather than Newcastle.</figcaption></figure>
<p>On paper, it was an implausible alliance.<sup id="cite_ref-65" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-65">&#91;65&#93;</a></sup> Pitt had been a strident critic of Newcastle for years, and they had separate, conflicting visions of strategy. Newcastle saw Britain's best chance of victory in directing resources to the war on the continent, but Pitt wanted a wholesale shift in policy to concentrate British forces in North America, West Africa and Asia, where the French were most vulnerable. However, they shared some views, were both ardent Whigs and had even once tried to create a political alliance. Newcastle had previously tried to have Pitt appointed Secretary of War in 1745, but George II vetoed the appointment.<sup id="cite_ref-66" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-66">&#91;66&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span id="Seven_Years.27_War"></span><span class="mw-headline" id="Seven_Years'_War">Seven Years' War</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Thomas_Pelham-Holles,_1st_Duke_of_Newcastle&amp;action=edit&amp;section=25" title="Edit section: Seven Years&#039; War">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<p>Ultimately British policies were formed from a mixture of these two views. Newcastle insisted on British involvement on the continent to tie down French troops and to authorise a number of expeditions against French colonies. As they were successful, the expeditions began to grow in number and size. Pitt largely took over control of directing them, and Newcastle agreed with the measures and made sure that <a href="/wiki/Parliament_of_Great_Britain" title="Parliament of Great Britain">Parliament</a> was kept on-side by mobilising his control of MPs. However, Pitt and Newcastle would discuss strategy along with a small number of other figures such as <a href="/wiki/Philip_Yorke,_1st_Earl_of_Hardwicke" title="Philip Yorke, 1st Earl of Hardwicke">Hardwicke</a>, <a href="/wiki/George_Anson,_1st_Baron_Anson" title="George Anson, 1st Baron Anson">Anson</a> and <a href="/wiki/Edward_Ligonier,_1st_Earl_Ligonier" title="Edward Ligonier, 1st Earl Ligonier">Ligonier</a>.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (February 2012)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p><p>Newcastle had been deeply concerned by Britain's poor start to the war, particularly by the loss of <a href="/wiki/Menorca" title="Menorca">Menorca</a> and the French occupation of key ports in the <a href="/wiki/Austrian_Netherlands" title="Austrian Netherlands">Austrian Netherlands</a>. To try to boost Britain's position in the Mediterranean, he pushed for an invasion of <a href="/wiki/Corsica" title="Corsica">Corsica</a>, which was then controlled by neutral <a href="/wiki/Republic_of_Genoa" title="Republic of Genoa">Genoa</a>, to use as a naval base or for a British attack on <a href="/wiki/Ostend" title="Ostend">Ostend</a> to drive the French out. Pitt was alarmed that both prospects would lead Britain into war with Austria or Genoa. Instead, to placate Newcastle and George II, Pitt agreed to send a British contingent to fight in Germany in 1758.
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Success">Success</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Thomas_Pelham-Holles,_1st_Duke_of_Newcastle&amp;action=edit&amp;section=26" title="Edit section: Success">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Annus_Mirabilis_of_1759" class="mw-redirect" title="Annus Mirabilis of 1759">Annus Mirabilis of 1759</a></div>
<p>In 1758, Pitt began despatching expeditions around the world to seize French colonies. In 1758, they captured <a href="/wiki/Senegal" title="Senegal">Senegal</a> and <a href="/wiki/Gambia" class="mw-redirect" title="Gambia">Gambia</a> in West Africa and <a href="/wiki/Louisbourg" title="Louisbourg">Louisbourg</a> in North America. He planned to intensify this the next year by despatching large expeditions to the <a href="/wiki/West_Indies" title="West Indies">West Indies</a> and Canada. To do so, Pitt stripped the <a href="/wiki/British_Isles" title="British Isles">British Isles</a> of troops and ships, which caused Newcastle to worry that they were ill-defended. His fears increased when the British received intelligence of French plans to launch an <a href="/wiki/Planned_French_Invasion_of_Britain_(1759)" class="mw-redirect" title="Planned French Invasion of Britain (1759)">invasion of Britain</a>. Pitt was determined to press ahead with that year's plans but agreed to lessen the scale of colonial expeditions for 1760 since he expected that 1759 would provide a knockout blow to the French war effort.
</p><p>Newcastle had retained his previous belief that Britain needed to create as broad a coalition as possible and that Europe, rather than the Americas, would be decisive. He thus attempted to persuade a number of different states to join the anti-French alliance. He was largely unsuccessful since the Dutch, Danes and Portuguese remained neutral, and Sweden and Russia joined the French and Austrians in attacking Prussia. He authorised large sums to be paid as subsidies to the Prussians, who were fighting countries whose land forces dwarfed their own.
</p><p>One of Newcastle's greatest personal achievements was his use of diplomacy to keep Spain out of the war until 1762, when it was too late to alter the balance of power significantly. In 1759, he and Pitt organised Britain's defences against a planned French invasion, which failed because of British naval victories at <a href="/wiki/Battle_of_Lagos" title="Battle of Lagos">Lagos</a> and <a href="/wiki/Battle_of_Quiberon_Bay" title="Battle of Quiberon Bay">Quiberon</a>.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Dismissal">Dismissal</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Thomas_Pelham-Holles,_1st_Duke_of_Newcastle&amp;action=edit&amp;section=27" title="Edit section: Dismissal">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>This "<a href="/wiki/Broad_bottom_government" title="Broad bottom government">broad bottom government</a>", under which Britain gained reputation abroad, gradually fell owing to the affection of the new king <a href="/wiki/George_III_of_the_United_Kingdom" class="mw-redirect" title="George III of the United Kingdom">George III</a> for <a href="/wiki/John_Stuart,_3rd_Earl_of_Bute" title="John Stuart, 3rd Earl of Bute">John Stuart, 3rd Earl of Bute</a>, who, having supplanted Pitt, became prime minister in place of Newcastle in May 1762.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEChisholm1911_1-5" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEChisholm1911-1">&#91;1&#93;</a></sup> George III had described Pitt as a "snake in the grass" and Newcastle as a "knave".<sup id="cite_ref-67" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-67">&#91;67&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Despite their undeniably competent prosecution of the war, the new king did not trust either man with the future of Britain and cast them both into opposition. It marked arguably the last occasion upon which a British monarch was able to remove a prime minister purely out of personal animosity: that privilege would in future be ceded entirely to Parliament. As Bute was a Tory, this marked the end of the Whig monopoly on government which had been continual since the Hanoverian Succession in 1714.
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Later_years">Later years</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Thomas_Pelham-Holles,_1st_Duke_of_Newcastle&amp;action=edit&amp;section=28" title="Edit section: Later years">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-left" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Claremont.JPG" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/03/Claremont.JPG/220px-Claremont.JPG" decoding="async" width="220" height="113" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/03/Claremont.JPG/330px-Claremont.JPG 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/03/Claremont.JPG/440px-Claremont.JPG 2x" data-file-width="2257" data-file-height="1161" /></a><figcaption>Newcastle spent much of his last few years at <a href="/wiki/Claremont_(country_house)" title="Claremont (country house)">Claremont</a>. He was exceptionally proud of the house and its <a href="/wiki/Claremont_Landscape_Garden" title="Claremont Landscape Garden">extensive gardens</a> on which he had spent a fortune.</figcaption></figure>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Opposition">Opposition</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Thomas_Pelham-Holles,_1st_Duke_of_Newcastle&amp;action=edit&amp;section=29" title="Edit section: Opposition">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>The Duke went into opposition and lost his two Lord-Lieutenancies for opposing the <a href="/wiki/Treaty_of_Paris_(1763)" title="Treaty of Paris (1763)">peace of 1763</a>. Along with Pitt, he felt the terms of peace were overly generous to France and Spain because of the position of strength held by the British. Many territories captured during the war were handed back, but the French presence had been effectively destroyed permanently in Canada and <a href="/wiki/Carnatic_Wars" title="Carnatic Wars">in India</a>.
</p><p>He spent much of his time at his house at <a href="/wiki/Claremont_(country_house)" title="Claremont (country house)">Claremont</a>, which he considered one of his finest achievements. Newcastle had been in government for almost 45 continuous years and initially enjoyed the new freedom that opposition gave him.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (February 2012)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Final_return">Final return</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Thomas_Pelham-Holles,_1st_Duke_of_Newcastle&amp;action=edit&amp;section=30" title="Edit section: Final return">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>In 1765, he became <a href="/wiki/Lord_Privy_Seal" title="Lord Privy Seal">Lord Privy Seal</a> in the government of <a href="/wiki/Charles_Watson-Wentworth,_2nd_Marquess_of_Rockingham" title="Charles Watson-Wentworth, 2nd Marquess of Rockingham">Lord Rockingham</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-68" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-68">&#91;68&#93;</a></sup> who shared many similarities with Newcastle, and both men were both wealthy Whig grandees. Newcastle was at one point offered the position of <a href="/wiki/Secretary_of_State_for_the_Southern_Department" title="Secretary of State for the Southern Department">Southern Secretary</a> by the king, but he turned it down. He lasted for a few months before the government collapsed, which was replaced by that of the <a href="/wiki/Augustus_FitzRoy,_3rd_Duke_of_Grafton" title="Augustus FitzRoy, 3rd Duke of Grafton">Duke of Grafton</a>.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Retirement">Retirement</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Thomas_Pelham-Holles,_1st_Duke_of_Newcastle&amp;action=edit&amp;section=31" title="Edit section: Retirement">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>He remained in active opposition but accepted he would not hold office again.<sup id="cite_ref-69" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-69">&#91;69&#93;</a></sup> He continued to wield enormous patronage and influence, but his health swiftly gave way after a stroke in December 1767, which left him lame and impaired in speech and memory.<sup id="cite_ref-70" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-70">&#91;70&#93;</a></sup> In his final few months, he had counselled against the <a href="/wiki/Coercive_Acts" class="mw-redirect" title="Coercive Acts">Coercive Acts</a> on <a href="/wiki/British_America" title="British America">British America</a>. He died in November 1768, aged 75, at his London home in <a href="/wiki/Lincoln%27s_Inn_Fields" title="Lincoln&#39;s Inn Fields">Lincoln's Inn Fields</a>. After his death, Claremont was sold to <a href="/wiki/Robert_Clive" title="Robert Clive">Robert Clive</a> who had made his name in the <a href="/wiki/Seven_Years%27_War" title="Seven Years&#39; War">Seven Years' War</a>.
</p><p>The Duke was industrious and energetic, and to his credit, the statesman who almost monopolised the patronage of office for half a century twice refused a pension and finally left office £300,000 poorer than he entered it<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEChisholm1911_1-6" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEChisholm1911-1">&#91;1&#93;</a></sup> because of his heavy spending on political campaigns, his lavish lifestyle and his neglect of the family budget.<sup id="cite_ref-71" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-71">&#91;71&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Legacy">Legacy</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Thomas_Pelham-Holles,_1st_Duke_of_Newcastle&amp;action=edit&amp;section=32" title="Edit section: Legacy">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-left" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Thomas_Pelham-Holles,_1st_Duke_of_Newcastle_KG.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/6f/Thomas_Pelham-Holles%2C_1st_Duke_of_Newcastle_KG.jpg/220px-Thomas_Pelham-Holles%2C_1st_Duke_of_Newcastle_KG.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="279" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/6f/Thomas_Pelham-Holles%2C_1st_Duke_of_Newcastle_KG.jpg/330px-Thomas_Pelham-Holles%2C_1st_Duke_of_Newcastle_KG.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/6f/Thomas_Pelham-Holles%2C_1st_Duke_of_Newcastle_KG.jpg/440px-Thomas_Pelham-Holles%2C_1st_Duke_of_Newcastle_KG.jpg 2x" data-file-width="750" data-file-height="950" /></a><figcaption>The Duke of Newcastle wearing the Robes of the <a href="/wiki/Order_of_the_Garter" title="Order of the Garter">Order of the Garter</a>, by <a href="/wiki/William_Hoare" title="William Hoare">William Hoare</a>, <abbr title="circa">c.</abbr><span style="white-space:nowrap;">&#8201;1750</span></figcaption></figure>
<p>Newcastle was widely caricatured, often being portrayed as a muddle-headed buffoon who struggled to understand the business of government. He was one of the most ridiculed politicians of the 18th century.<sup id="cite_ref-72" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-72">&#91;72&#93;</a></sup> A common and widely circulated example of his cluelessness is his reported response to being told by <a href="/wiki/John_Ligonier,_1st_Earl_Ligonier" title="John Ligonier, 1st Earl Ligonier">Ligonier</a> that <a href="/wiki/Annapolis_Royal,_Nova_Scotia" class="mw-redirect" title="Annapolis Royal, Nova Scotia">Annapolis</a> needed to be defended, to which Newcastle allegedly replied, "Annapolis! Oh yes, Annapolis must be defended, to be sure. Annapolis must be defended—where is Annapolis?".<sup id="cite_ref-pm88_73-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-pm88-73">&#91;73&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-74" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-74">&#91;74&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p><a href="/wiki/Horace_Walpole" title="Horace Walpole">Horace Walpole</a>, no friend of him, sketched his character thus: "A borrowed importance and real insignificance gave him the perpetual air of a solicitor.... He had no pride, though infinite self-love. He loved business immoderately; yet was only always doing it, never did it. When left to himself, he always plunged into difficulties, and then shuddered for the consequences."<sup id="cite_ref-pm88_73-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-pm88-73">&#91;73&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Historical opinion has generally been divided, with some historians drawing the conclusion that he was unfit for his office, but others regard him as a shrewd political operator who subtly navigated the complex European State System of the 18th century. He is both praised and criticised as being perhaps the greatest machine politics operator of the 18th century, who commanded immense voting strength in parliament. He could often organise majorities in the House of Commons for seemingly perplexing, unpopular and absurd policies of the government.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (February 2012)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p><p>Generally, praise for Britain's victory in the Seven Years' War has gone to Pitt rather than Newcastle, who officially headed the government. Traditionally, accounts of the war have portrayed Pitt as a visionary who won the war by reversing Newcastle's previous unwise policy of focusing on European affairs; <a href="/wiki/Francis_Parkman" title="Francis Parkman">Francis Parkman</a> records correspondence between Pitt and his military administrators and none between them and Newcastle. Others have defended Newcastle by contrasting his 'continental policy' with <a href="/wiki/Lord_North" class="mw-redirect" title="Lord North">Lord North</a>'s failure to gather European allies during the <a href="/wiki/American_War_of_Independence" class="mw-redirect" title="American War of Independence">American War of Independence</a>, which led to Britain's eventual defeat in this conflict.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (February 2012)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Popular_culture">Popular culture</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Thomas_Pelham-Holles,_1st_Duke_of_Newcastle&amp;action=edit&amp;section=33" title="Edit section: Popular culture">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>He was portrayed in the novel <i><a href="/wiki/The_Expedition_of_Humphry_Clinker" title="The Expedition of Humphry Clinker">Humphry Clinker</a></i> by <a href="/wiki/Tobias_Smollett" title="Tobias Smollett">Tobias Smollett</a> as a bungling fool, ignorant of all geography, who is convinced that <a href="/wiki/Cape_Breton_Island" title="Cape Breton Island">Cape Breton</a> is not an island.<sup id="cite_ref-75" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-75">&#91;75&#93;</a></sup> Newcastle was played in the 1948 film <i><a href="/wiki/Bonnie_Prince_Charlie_(1948_film)" title="Bonnie Prince Charlie (1948 film)">Bonnie Prince Charlie</a></i> by <a href="/wiki/G._H._Mulcaster" title="G. H. Mulcaster">G. H. Mulcaster</a>. He also features in the British television series <i><a href="/wiki/City_of_Vice" title="City of Vice">City of Vice</a></i>, which covers the early years of the <a href="/wiki/Bow_Street_Runners" title="Bow Street Runners">Bow Street Runners</a>.
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Family">Family</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Thomas_Pelham-Holles,_1st_Duke_of_Newcastle&amp;action=edit&amp;section=34" title="Edit section: Family">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Thomas_Pelham-Holles_(1693-1768).jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/22/Thomas_Pelham-Holles_%281693-1768%29.jpg/170px-Thomas_Pelham-Holles_%281693-1768%29.jpg" decoding="async" width="170" height="197" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/22/Thomas_Pelham-Holles_%281693-1768%29.jpg/255px-Thomas_Pelham-Holles_%281693-1768%29.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/2/22/Thomas_Pelham-Holles_%281693-1768%29.jpg 2x" data-file-width="305" data-file-height="354" /></a><figcaption>Thomas Pelham-Holles, 1st Duke of Newcastle-upon-Tyne</figcaption></figure>
<p>On 2 April 1717, he married <a href="/wiki/Harriet_Pelham-Holles,_Duchess_of_Newcastle-upon-Tyne" class="mw-redirect" title="Harriet Pelham-Holles, Duchess of Newcastle-upon-Tyne">Lady Harriet Godolphin</a>, daughter of the <a href="/wiki/Francis_Godolphin,_2nd_Earl_of_Godolphin" title="Francis Godolphin, 2nd Earl of Godolphin">2nd Earl of Godolphin</a> and granddaughter of <a href="/wiki/John_Churchill,_1st_Duke_of_Marlborough" title="John Churchill, 1st Duke of Marlborough">John Churchill, 1st Duke of Marlborough</a>. The Duchess suffered from poor health and the couple had no children.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (October 2020)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p><p>In 1731, at <a href="/wiki/Houghton_Hall" title="Houghton Hall">Houghton Hall</a>, Sir Robert Walpole's <a href="/wiki/Country_house" class="mw-redirect" title="Country house">country house</a> in Norfolk, the Duke, with the <a href="/wiki/Francis_I,_Holy_Roman_Emperor" title="Francis I, Holy Roman Emperor">Duke of Lorraine</a> (later the <a href="/wiki/Holy_Roman_Emperor" title="Holy Roman Emperor">Holy Roman Emperor</a>), was made a Master Mason by the Grand Master, Lord Lovell, at an Occasional Lodge.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (January 2009)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> In 1739, at the creation of London's <a href="/wiki/Foundling_Hospital" title="Foundling Hospital">Foundling Hospital</a>, he acted as one of the charity's founding governors.<sup id="cite_ref-76" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-76">&#91;76&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Succession">Succession</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Thomas_Pelham-Holles,_1st_Duke_of_Newcastle&amp;action=edit&amp;section=35" title="Edit section: Succession">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>With the prospect that the dukedom of Newcastle upon Tyne would become extinct once again, <a href="/wiki/George_II_of_Great_Britain" title="George II of Great Britain">King George II</a> also created the <b>Duke of Newcastle-under-Lyne</b> in 1756, with a special remainder for inheritance through his nephew, the <a href="/wiki/Henry_Pelham-Clinton,_2nd_Duke_of_Newcastle-under-Lyne" class="mw-redirect" title="Henry Pelham-Clinton, 2nd Duke of Newcastle-under-Lyne">9th Earl of Lincoln</a>.
</p><p>In 1762, he was also created <b>Baron Pelham of Stanmer</b>, with inheritance to his cousin and male heir, <a href="/wiki/Thomas_Pelham,_1st_Earl_of_Chichester" title="Thomas Pelham, 1st Earl of Chichester">Thomas Pelham</a>.
</p><p>On his death in 1768, the title Baron Pelham of Stanmer, together with the bulk of the Pelham estates in Sussex and the Duke's private papers, were left to Thomas, who was later created <a href="/wiki/Earl_of_Chichester" title="Earl of Chichester">Earl of Chichester</a>. Pelham and his brother were buried at All Saints' Church in <a href="/wiki/Laughton,_East_Sussex" title="Laughton, East Sussex">Laughton, East Sussex</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-ThPelODNB_77-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ThPelODNB-77">&#91;77&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The Holles and Clare estates, meanwhile, together with his Newcastle dukedom, were inherited by Lord Lincoln from whom the Duke had become estranged.
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Ancestry">Ancestry</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Thomas_Pelham-Holles,_1st_Duke_of_Newcastle&amp;action=edit&amp;section=36" title="Edit section: Ancestry">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>

<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Arms">Arms</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Thomas_Pelham-Holles,_1st_Duke_of_Newcastle&amp;action=edit&amp;section=37" title="Edit section: Arms">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<table class="wikitable mw-collapsible" style="max-width: 100%">
<caption class="nowrap">Coat of arms of Thomas Pelham-Holles, 1st Duke of Newcastle
</caption>
<tbody><tr>
<td style="width: 220px;"><span class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Frameless"><a href="/wiki/File:Coat_of_Arms_of_Thomas_Pelham-Holles,_1st_Duke_of_Newcastle.svg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/91/Coat_of_Arms_of_Thomas_Pelham-Holles%2C_1st_Duke_of_Newcastle.svg/220px-Coat_of_Arms_of_Thomas_Pelham-Holles%2C_1st_Duke_of_Newcastle.svg.png" decoding="async" width="220" height="262" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/91/Coat_of_Arms_of_Thomas_Pelham-Holles%2C_1st_Duke_of_Newcastle.svg/330px-Coat_of_Arms_of_Thomas_Pelham-Holles%2C_1st_Duke_of_Newcastle.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/91/Coat_of_Arms_of_Thomas_Pelham-Holles%2C_1st_Duke_of_Newcastle.svg/440px-Coat_of_Arms_of_Thomas_Pelham-Holles%2C_1st_Duke_of_Newcastle.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="385" data-file-height="459" /></a></span>
</td>
<td style="min-width:175px;">
<dl><dt>Crest</dt>
<dd>A Peacock in pride Proper</dd>
<dt>Escutcheon</dt>
<dd>Quarterly of six, 1st and 6th grandquarterly Azure three Pelicans vulning themselves Proper and Ermine two Piles in point Sable; 2nd, Gules two Demi-belts Argent with Buckles Or erect (the augmenting buckles recognising the capture of King John of France (Jean II le Bon) by Sir John Pelham); 3rd, Ermine on a Fess Gules three open Crowns Or; 4th, Azure fretty Or; 5th, Azure two Bars Argent on a Canton Sable a Wolf's Head erased of the Second.</dd>
<dt>Motto</dt>
<dd>Loyaulté n'a honte (No shame in loyalty)</dd></dl>
</td></tr></tbody></table>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Notes">Notes</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Thomas_Pelham-Holles,_1st_Duke_of_Newcastle&amp;action=edit&amp;section=38" title="Edit section: Notes">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1011085734">.mw-parser-output .reflist{font-size:90%;margin-bottom:0.5em;list-style-type:decimal}.mw-parser-output .reflist .references{font-size:100%;margin-bottom:0;list-style-type:inherit}.mw-parser-output .reflist-columns-2{column-width:30em}.mw-parser-output .reflist-columns-3{column-width:25em}.mw-parser-output .reflist-columns{margin-top:0.3em}.mw-parser-output .reflist-columns ol{margin-top:0}.mw-parser-output .reflist-columns li{page-break-inside:avoid;break-inside:avoid-column}.mw-parser-output .reflist-upper-alpha{list-style-type:upper-alpha}.mw-parser-output .reflist-upper-roman{list-style-type:upper-roman}.mw-parser-output .reflist-lower-alpha{list-style-type:lower-alpha}.mw-parser-output .reflist-lower-greek{list-style-type:lower-greek}.mw-parser-output .reflist-lower-roman{list-style-type:lower-roman}</style><div class="reflist">
<div class="mw-references-wrap mw-references-columns"><ol class="references">
